  D) Undefined

Question 286 (ID: 289):
If a string of 8 bits undergoes a right circular shift (rotate right) of 2 bits, what happens to the bits?

Options:
  A) The rightmost 2 bits wrap around to become the leftmost 2 bits [CORRECT ANSWER]
  B) The rightmost 2 bits are lost and replaced with 0s
  C) The leftmost 2 bits wrap around to become the rightmost 2 bits
  D) All bits are inverted

Verified Answer: The rightmost 2 bits wrap around to become the leftmost 2 bits
Explanation: In a circular shift (rotation), bits that fall off one end are recirculated to the opposite end.

  D) None

Question 1072 (ID: 1075):
By using 16 bits in 2's complement notation, what is the maximum positive integer that can be represented?

Options:
  A) 32767 [CORRECT ANSWER]
  B) 65535
  C) 32768
  D) 16384

Verified Answer: 32767
Explanation: In 16-bit 2's complement notation, the range is -2^15 to +(2^15 - 1) = -32,768 to +32,767.

  B) 1 with no carry
  C) 2 with a carry of 1
  D) 0 with no carry

Verified Answer: 0 with a carry of 1 (10 in binary)
Explanation: In base 2, 1 + 1 = 10_2 (which is 0 with a carry of 1 into the next higher bit).

Dynamic RAM (DRAM) stores each bit of data as an electric charge in a capacitor, which requires:

  C) Constant mechanical rotation
  D) No electrical power once written

Verified Answer: Periodic refreshing thousands of times per second
Explanation: Capacitors leak charge, so Dynamic RAM requires continuous periodic refresh cycles.

Question 1284 (ID: 1287):
In the 24-bit True Color RGB color model, each of the Red, Green, and Blue color components is encoded using:

Options:
  A) 8 bits (1 byte) per component, giving 256 intensity levels [CORRECT ANSWER]
  B) 1 bit per component
  C) 16 bytes per component
  D) 32 bits per component

Verified Answer: 8 bits (1 byte) per component, giving 256 intensity levels
Explanation: 24-bit True Color allocates 8 bits (256 levels) to Red, 8 bits to Green, and 8 bits to Blue (2^24 = 16.7M colors).

Question 1332 (ID: 1335):
The Atanasoff-Berry Computer (ABC), developed between 1939 and 1942, was specifically designed to solve:

Options:
  A) Systems of simultaneous linear equations [CORRECT ANSWER]
  B) Artillery trajectory ballistic firing tables
  C) Word processing and desktop publishing
  D) Spreadsheet financial calculations

Verified Answer: Systems of simultaneous linear equations
Explanation: The ABC was built by John Atanasoff and Clifford Berry to solve up to 29 simultaneous linear algebraic equations.

Question 1402 (ID: 1405):
In computer architecture, which instructions are used to transfer data between CPU registers and external peripheral devices?

Options:
  A) I/O (Input/Output) Instructions [CORRECT ANSWER]
  B) Arithmetic Instructions
  C) Logic Branch Instructions
  D) Floating-point Rounding Instructions

Verified Answer: I/O (Input/Output) Instructions
Explanation: I/O instructions transfer bytes between processor registers and I/O ports or memory-mapped device controllers.

Question 1412 (ID: 1415):
In modern multitasking operating systems, running multiple programs concurrently on a single CPU core is achieved by:

Options:
  A) Time-sharing (Time-slicing) the CPU rapidly between active processes [CORRECT ANSWER]
  B) Adding physical hard disks for each application
  C) Halving the electrical voltage to the monitor
  D) Running programs only after midnight

Verified Answer: Time-sharing (Time-slicing) the CPU rapidly between active processes
Explanation: Multitasking OSs allocate tiny time slices to each process, giving the user the illusion of simultaneous execution.
